# **ChainTorrent MVP**

## Problem Statement

JavaScript dependency distribution runs through a single centralized registry that is an availability chokepoint, a mutable source of truth, and an intermediary between the people who publish packages and the people who consume them. ChainTorrent's MVP replaces that path with a content-addressed swarm, a canonical on-chain identity registry, and transferable access entitlements, so that the distribution and identity model is proven with monetization out of bounds.

**Adoption runs individual developer first, and everything else follows from that.** A developer at a terminal gains cross-project reuse of whatever their machine has already fetched, swarm retrieval on popular packages, and installs that survive a registry outage. Build platforms adopt next and for their own reasons: a service running the same few thousand popular installs continuously, on always-on machines with real bandwidth, holds the ideal cache as a by-product of its own economics, and seeding that cache costs almost nothing once it exists. That makes them natural superseeders rather than participants anyone has to recruit, and it is why no privileged bootstrap host is part of this plan. CI and enterprise adoption arrive as a consequence of that sequence rather than as targets to be won early.

The sequence matters to this workplan because it determines what the MVP measures. Latency is judged against interactive local installs rather than pipeline tolerance, since a developer notices a doubled install where a pipeline does not. Cache reuse is the headline adoption metric, because it is the benefit the adopting population actually experiences. Registry-outage survival remains true throughout and is simply not the lead, since it matters most to the population that adopts last.

The design is specified in `docs/research/cryptography.md` and bounded in `docs/research/MVP Scope.md`. Undetermined decisions are held in the To-Do list below.

The cryptographic construction is specified; its research record is `docs/research/cryptography-research-notebook.md`, and its statement is `docs/research/cryptography-critical-path.md`. A validation harness on both pairing curves produces the sizes, timings, and gas against the configured piece-group size and curve defaults, and nothing waits on it: every node reads the piece-group size from the deployment record and depends only on the code it calls.

## Objectives

Deliver an install path for JavaScript dependencies that survives registry outages, serves popular packages from a peer swarm, and exercises the identity and entitlement pipeline end to end.

Prove distribution and identity. Willingness to pay and seeder compensation are out of bounds and are held in the To-Do list; the transaction flow is proven at a nominal price on assets the project publishes, as a test instrument rather than a monetization step.

Every undetermined decision is carried in the To-Do list, which every workplan that stems from this one inherits.

## Expected Outcome

A developer installs dependencies and receives a working `node_modules` assembled from a local content-addressed store, a peer swarm, or the ingest source as fallback, with canonical identity resolved on-chain and an access entitlement minted to their wallet.

* `[✅]`   `proof/mint_statement` **Mint transcript under delivery-statement version one, the suite identifier, chain, entitlement contract, asset root, parameter-set digest, source and target entitlements, old and new interval counters, purpose, issuer, buyer, buyer's envelope keys, new envelope, expiry, and the mint relation's first messages, with its canonical description for the encoding contract and the delivery purposes as declared data; creates the `adapters/proof` crate**

  * `[✅]`   `objective`
    * `[✅]`   Problem: the delivery proof's challenge is the hash-to-scalar mapping of the transcript, canonically encoded under the delivery-statement version the hash-card names, and the contract recomputes it, so a proof is valid for exactly one settlement; the mint transcript carries the suite identifier, the chain, the entitlement contract, the asset root, the parameter-set digest, the source and target entitlements, the old and new interval counters, the purpose, the issuer's identity in the seller field, the buyer's identity, the buyer's envelope keys, the new envelope, the expiry, and the mint relation's first messages, each group element as its precompile encoding; the chain, entitlement, interval, and identity fields take the forms the hash-card's forms identifier names, which the deployment's entitlement-state adapter declares it binds, the entitlement contract taking the identity form; the purpose is mint, transfer, grant, or replacement as the unsigned sixteen-bit codes 1, 2, 3, and 4, mint and replacement proving the mint relation and transfer and grant the transfer relation; so the transcript is a value with one canonical field sequence the encoding factory encodes, generic over the chain's forms, each form yielding and rebuilt from one canonical field (CR-09; CD-01; `docs/research/cryptography.md`'s Delivery Proof statement and its transcript per delivery-statement version; `docs/research/MVP Scope.md`'s Native Credentials; the dependency map's `proof/mint_statement` row; the Delivery proof and on-chain verifier milestone; the product requirements' canonical encoding position)
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the delivery purposes are declared data: mint with code `1` and the mint relation, transfer with code `2` and the transfer relation, grant with code `3` and the transfer relation, replacement with code `4` and the mint relation, in that order
    * `[✅]`   Functional: one type holds the mint transcript over the selected pairing and any chain's forms: the suite identifier and the parameter-set digest as their domain types, the asset root as the hashing family's `Root`, the chain, entitlement contract, entitlements, interval counters, issuer, and buyer as the chain's forms, the purpose as `MintPurpose`, and the buyer's envelope keys, new envelope, and first messages as typed pairing points, and the expiry as an unsigned sixty-four-bit value
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the transcript's description states its canonical fields in transcript order, maps a transcript to its canonical field values, and maps canonical field values back to a transcript or to the refusal that names what failed and where
    * `[✅]`   Functional: mapping fields back reads the field list through the encoding family's canonical-field reader over the description's `FIELDS`, which refuses a list of the wrong length before any field is read, and a field of the wrong kind or a form's refusal with its index, the reader's refusal carried unchanged; the fields are read in ascending index, the lowest failing index deciding among the reader's refusals, a domain type's refusal, a purpose code outside the mint relation, and an invalid group encoding
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the reference transcript maps to the reference fields and the reference fields map back to the reference transcript
    * `[✅]`   Non-functional: the proof crate depends on `adapters/encoding`, `adapters/chain`, `adapters/pairing`, `adapters/envelope`, `adapters/hashing`, and `crates/domain`; nothing in the crate names a curve, a hash, a format, a chain, or a vendor

  * `[✅]`   `role`
    * `[✅]`   Adapter family: the delivery-proof family's family-owned description of the mint transcript, and the family's first source file, so it creates the `adapters/proof` crate; the transcript and its description live in the proof family because the transcript is the delivery proof's statement
    * `[✅]`   Consumer of the encoding family's canonical-field contract and canonical-field reader and the chain family's form interface; it reads a form's field kind and converts a form to one canonical field value through the contract, reads each canonical field and rebuilds each form through the reader, and obtains the forms only through `chain/create_chain_forms`
    * `[✅]`   Maps the suite identifier, the asset root, and the parameter-set digest directly, as the derivation context's description maps its components; only the chain's forms pass through the canonical-field contract
    * `[✅]`   The description encodes typed group elements through the selected pairing and validates decoded group encodings before constructing a trusted statement
    * `[✅]`   `MintPurpose` and `TransferPurpose` encode the relation in their types; wire reconstruction refuses a purpose code from the wrong relation
    * `[✅]`   Does not produce the transcript's bytes or hash them; the encoding factory encodes the transcript and `proof/schnorr_fs/challenge` maps the encoding to the challenge
    * `[✅]`   Does not supply any chain's forms; `chain/evm_forms` supplies the EVM suite's as a private concrete, the family-owned function `chain/create_chain_forms` hands them to a consumer by the identifier the suite requires, and `chain/base` binds them
    * `[✅]`   Does not create the proof family's `factory` module; `proof/schnorr_fs` authors the family's generic interface, declaration, and mock there
    * `[✅]`   Does not carry a commit

  * `[✅]`   `objective`

* `[✅]`   `proof/schnorr_fs/challenge` **Fiat–Shamir challenge of the delivery proof, the hash-to-scalar mapping under the proof's domain tag of a mint or transfer transcript's encoding through the injected encoder and hash-to-scalar adapter; creates the `schnorr_fs` module**

  * `[✅]`   `objective`
    * `[✅]`   Problem: the delivery proof is made non-interactive by Fiat–Shamir over the full statement; its challenge is the hash-to-scalar mapping, under the proof's domain tag and through the concrete the hash-card names, of the transcript, which carries the statement fields, the settlement context, and the prover's first messages, canonically encoded under the delivery-statement version the hash-card names; the contract recomputes it, so a proof is valid for exactly one settlement; every value a contract recomputes is the hash-to-scalar mapping under a domain tag, keccak256 in the MVP suite, and the construction is fixed at this ticket and mirrored in Solidity through the generated vectors (CR-09; `docs/research/cryptography.md`'s Delivery Proof statement; `docs/project-planning/tech-stack.md`'s hash-to-scalar and challenge row; the dependency map's `proof/schnorr_fs/challenge` row; the Delivery proof and on-chain verifier milestone)
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the challenge of a mint transcript is the injected hash-to-scalar adapter's mapping, under the proof's domain tag, of the injected encoder's encoding of that transcript through `MintStatementDescription`
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the challenge of a transfer transcript, a sale's or a grant's, is the same mapping of the encoder's encoding of that transcript through `TransferStatementDescription`
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the same transcript always yields the same challenge, and a transcript differing in any field yields another
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the proof's domain tag is a constant the function states, admitted by the hash-to-scalar family's tag type, which the generate family mirrors to Solidity
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a hash-to-scalar refusal is returned unchanged in the function's own error
    * `[✅]`   Non-functional: every encoding passes through `IEncoderAdapter` and every mapping through `IHashToScalarAdapter`; the module names no curve, hash, encoding, or vendor

    * `[✅]`   Constructs the transcript's description itself, `MintStatementDescription` for a mint transcript and `TransferStatementDescription` for a transfer transcript, over the transcript's chain forms, and hands it to the injected encoder
    * `[✅]`   States the proof's domain tag as a constant and constructs it through the hash-to-scalar family's `DomainTag`, as `kem/bb1_depth_one` and `envelope/pairing_elgamal` state theirs
    * `[✅]`   Does not choose the encoding or hash-to-scalar concrete; the caller holds the encoder the encoding factory hands its consumer and the hash-to-scalar adapter the hash-to-scalar factory returns for its pairing's scalar type, each the concrete the hash-card's identifiers name
    * `[✅]`   Does not build a transcript, compute a first message or a response, choose a purpose's relation, or verify a proof; `proof/schnorr_fs` does
    * `[✅]`   Does not carry a commit

* `[✅]`   `proof/schnorr_fs` **Generalized Schnorr delivery-proof concrete over any pairing concrete: the mint prover, the transfer prover from the seller's fresh decryption and total offset, and the reference verifier in both verifier forms, second-group arithmetic and the hash-weighted pairing product with first-group arithmetic only, the form fixed at construction; authors the delivery-proof family's generic interface, algebraic statement types, context, proof components, declaration, and mock**

  * `[✅]`   `objective`
    * `[✅]`   Problem: the settlement contract accepts an envelope only with a proof that it decrypts under the recipient's registered keys to a valid credential for the exact entitlement; the mint proof shows knowledge of `(α, r, ρ, σ)` with `hpub = g2^α`, `C1 = g1^ρ`, `C2 = g1^α · F_I^r · pk1^ρ`, `D1 = g2^σ`, and `D2 = g2^r · pk2^σ`; the transfer proof shows, against the previous interval's envelope and the seller's registered keys, knowledge of `(x_s, y_s, s, ρ, σ)` with `pk1_s = g1^x_s`, `C1_n = g1^ρ`, `C2_n / C2_o = C1_o^(−x_s) · F_I^s · pk1_b^ρ`, `pk2_s = g2^y_s`, `D1_n = g2^σ`, and `D2_n / D2_o = D1_o^(−y_s) · g2^s · pk2_b^σ`, only the total offset entering; the proof is generalized Schnorr made non-interactive by Fiat–Shamir over the full transcript; with second-group arithmetic at the verifier it is the challenge and one response per witness, verified by sixteen source-group scalar multiplications in six multi-scalar multiplications and no pairing; with first-group arithmetic only, the three second-group first messages travel too and the second-group equations are checked as one hash-weighted pairing product, ten pairs for a transfer and eight for a mint, because an unweighted product is not equivalent to the separate equalities and a crafted proof passes it; the Rust verifier matches the deployed verifier bit for bit (CR-09; CD-01; CD-02; `docs/research/cryptography.md`'s Delivery Proof statement; `docs/research/cryptography-critical-path.md`'s construction; `docs/research/cryptography-research-notebook.md`'s chained source-group proof and sizes; the risk register's unweighted-product risk; the dependency map's `proof/schnorr_fs` row; the Delivery proof and on-chain verifier milestone)
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the mint prover takes the master scalar, the credential's randomness `r`, the envelope's coins `(ρ, σ)`, and the algebraic mint statement, draws one nonce per witness, forms the mint relation's five first messages, computes the challenge over the mint transcript, and returns the challenge and the responses `(z_α, z_r, z_ρ, z_σ)`, each `z = k + c · w`
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the transfer prover takes the seller's key-pair secrets `(x_s, y_s)`, the total offset `s`, the new envelope's coins `(ρ, σ)`, and the algebraic transfer statement, and returns the challenge and the responses `(z_x, z_y, z_s, z_ρ, z_σ)` over the transfer relation's six first messages and the transfer transcript
    * `[✅]`   Functional: each prover refuses a statement whose purpose proves the other relation
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the verifier in the both-groups form recomputes every first message from the responses, the challenge, and the statement, recomputes the challenge over the transcript, and accepts exactly when it equals the proof's
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the verifier in the first-group-only form recomputes the first-group first messages, takes the second-group first messages from the proof, recomputes the challenge and requires it equal, and checks the three second-group equations as one pairing product, the first equation weighted one and the second and third each weighted by the hash-to-scalar mapping, under the weight tag, of the challenge's and every response's canonical bytes in order followed by the equation's one-byte position
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the verifier rejects a statement whose purpose proves the other relation, a proof of the other form, and a proof with the wrong number of responses, and a proof whose second-group errors cancel in an unweighted product
    * `[✅]`   Functional: every proof exposes its components, the challenge, the responses in witness order, and the second-group first messages the form carries, and is rebuilt from them through a fallible constructor refusing a count of second-group first messages the form does not carry
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the concrete declares the envelope algebras, verifier forms, and delivery-statement versions it supports, the domain tags its challenge and its pairing-product weights hash under, its adapter version, and the interface version it implements, readable before any instance exists, so a consumer that mirrors the proof reads the tags from the declaration and names no concrete
    * `[✅]`   Non-functional: every source-group, scalar, and pairing operation passes through `IPairingArithmetic`, every challenge through `challenge`, every weight through `IHashToScalarAdapter`, and every nonce through `IRandomSourceAdapter`; the crate names no curve, hash, encoding, or randomness library; witnesses and nonces are held in types that zeroize on drop

  * `[✅]`   `construction`
    * `[✅]`   `SchnorrFsDeliveryProof::try_new` is the concrete's only producer, and its only caller is the delivery-proof factory, which reads `SchnorrFsDeliveryProof::<'_, P, E, F>::DECLARATION` before constructing and constructs it from borrows of the resolved pairing concrete, the hash-to-scalar adapter for that pairing's scalar type, the encoder the encoding factory hands its consumer, and the randomness source the random factory returns, all held by the factory's caller, with the verifier form the resolved pairing declares
    * `[✅]`   Proofs are produced only by the provers and by `proof_from_components`; statements and contexts are built by their consumers from the credential KEM's and key agreement's components and the settlement's context, and by the family's builders in tests; the concrete's `mock.rs` is `#[cfg(test)]`, its collaborators are the families' mock concretes reached through their factories, and a test builds the adapter, the statements, the payloads, and the proofs through the builders above; the tests' composition of the pairing, chain-forms, encoding, hash-to-scalar, and randomness families runs through `run_schnorr_fs_case`, whose levels make the factory calls and invoke each block's `ISchnorrFsCaseConsumer` once; the adapter itself is injected and is mocked through `MockIDeliveryProofAdapter`

    * `[✅]`   `impl<'a, P: IPairingArithmetic, E: IEncoderAdapter, F: IChainForms> IDeliveryProofAdapter for SchnorrFsDeliveryProof<'a, P, E, F>` with `const DECLARATION: DeliveryProofDeclaration = SchnorrFsDeliveryProof::DECLARATION;`, `type Pairing = P;`, `type Forms = F;`, and `type Proof = SchnorrFsProof<P>;`, and every method realizing its branches and ordering in the interaction spec, each pairing call on `self.pairing`, each challenge through `challenge`, each weight on `self.hash_to_scalar`, and each draw on `self.random`
    * `[✅]`   Imports the family's names from `crate::factory::provides`, `MintStatement`, `TransferStatement`, `DeliveryRelation`, and `DELIVERY_STATEMENT_VERSION_ONE` from `crate::mint_statement::provides` and `crate::transfer_statement::provides`, `challenge`, `ChallengeDeps`, `ChallengeParams`, `ChallengePayload`, `ChallengeTranscript`, and `SCHNORR_FS_CHALLENGE_TAG` from `challenge::provides`, `Secret` from `domain`, the pairing traits, params, payloads, and term types from `pairing`, the hash-to-scalar names from `hash_to_scalar`, `IEncoderAdapter` from `encoding`, `IRandomSourceAdapter`, `FillBytesParams`, and `FillBytesPayload` from `random`, `EnvelopeAlgebra` from `envelope`, `IChainForms` from `chain`, `core::marker::PhantomData`, and this module's names from `interface`
